\mathcal{U}_{l,j}^{k}\triangleq\left\{\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+\mu_{j,k}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})\big|\geqslant 2\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\right\},

having used the fact that
\gamma=\varepsilon^{2+\delta}.
Notice that

\bigcap_{(l,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\atop k\leqslant|j|}\mathcal{U}_{l,j}^{k}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{k}.

Moreover, by continuity and non-degeneracy assumption on the period
3.17
, we infer

\forall\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}],\quad 0<\overline{b}\leqslant\omega(\lambda)\leqslant\overline{a},\qquad\overline{a}\triangleq\max_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]}\omega_{0}(\lambda),\qquad\overline{b}\triangleq\min_{\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]}\omega_{0}(\lambda).

Moreover, from (
7.83
), for all
0<\varepsilon\leqslant\varepsilon_{0}
one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{3}\leqslant\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})\leqslant\tfrac{2}{3}\cdot

(7.124)

We distinguish different cases.

\bullet
Case
\varepsilon^{2}\overline{b}|l|\geqslant|j|\geqslant k
:
For
k=1
, from the triangle inequality, we get

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\ell+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\varepsilon^{2}\overline{b}|l|-\tfrac{2}{3}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{1}{3}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\cdot

Similarly, for
k=2
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})-\tfrac{j}{2|j|}\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\varepsilon^{2}\overline{b}|l|-\tfrac{2}{3}|j|-\tfrac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{1}{3}|j|-\tfrac{1}{2}\geqslant\tfrac{1}{6}

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\cdot

Therefore, for
k\in\{1,2\},

\mathcal{U}_{l,j}^{k}=[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}].

\bullet
Case
|j|\geqslant 24\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|
: For
k=1
, one gets by the triangle inequality and (
7.124
), together with
|j|\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{1}{3}|j|-\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|

\displaystyle\geqslant\big(\tfrac{1}{24}|j|-\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|\big)+\tfrac{7}{24}|j|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{7}{24}\cdot

Thus, for small
\varepsilon
we infer

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\cdot

For
k=2
, one obtains by the triangle inequality and (
7.124
), together with
|j|\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})-\tfrac{j}{2|j|}\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{1}{3}|j|-\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|-\tfrac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\big(\tfrac{1}{24}|j|-\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|\big)+\tfrac{7}{24}|j|-\tfrac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{1}{12}\cdot

Then, for small
\varepsilon
we infer

\displaystyle\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})-\tfrac{j}{2|j|}\big|

\displaystyle\geqslant\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\cdot

Hence, for
k\in\{1,2\},

\mathcal{U}_{l,j}^{k}=[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}].

Consequently, one has

\displaystyle\bigcap_{|j|\leqslant 24\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|\atop\varepsilon^{2}\overline{b}|l|\leqslant|j|}\mathcal{U}_{l,j}^{k}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{k}.

(7.125)

Set

f_{l,j}(\lambda)\triangleq\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})-(k-1)\tfrac{j}{2|j|}\cdot

Differentiating
f_{l,j}
with respect to
\lambda
and using (
7.83
) give

f_{l,j}^{\prime}(\lambda)=\varepsilon^{2}\omega^{\prime}(\lambda)\,l+\varepsilon^{3}j\mathtt{c}_{2}^{\prime}(\lambda).

By non-degeneracy assumption and from the estimate (
7.84
), we conclude that

\forall\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}],\quad 0<\underline{c}\leqslant|\omega^{\prime}(\lambda)|\quad\hbox{and}\quad|\mathtt{c}_{2}^{\prime}(\lambda)|\leqslant c_{1}.

Since
|j|\leqslant 24\varepsilon^{2}\overline{a}|l|,
then for all
\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}],
we have

\displaystyle|f_{j,l}^{\prime}(\lambda)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\underline{c}\varepsilon^{2}|l|-\varepsilon^{3}|j|c_{1}.

\displaystyle\geqslant|j|\big(\tfrac{\underline{c}}{24\overline{a}}-c_{1}\varepsilon^{3}\big).

Thus, for
\varepsilon
small enough,

\displaystyle|f_{j,l}^{\prime}(\lambda)|\geqslant\tfrac{\underline{c}}{48\overline{a}}|j|.

Applying Lemma
7.16
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\big|\varepsilon^{2}\omega(\lambda)\,l+j\mathtt{c}(\lambda,\rho_{\infty})-(k-1)\tfrac{j}{2|j|}\big|<2\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{\tau}}\right\}\right|\leqslant C\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{1+\tau}}\cdot

Consequently, from (
7.125
), we get

\displaystyle\big|[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]\setminus\mathcal{C}_{\infty}^{k}\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\sum_{\varepsilon^{2}\overline{b}|\ell|\leqslant|j|}\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2+\delta}}{|j|^{1+\tau}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{\delta}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\tfrac{1}{|j|^{\tau}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\varepsilon^{\delta},

proving (
7.123
).
It is important to mention that we are slightly stretching the argument here, as the function
f_{j,l}
is Lipschitz continuous rather than
C^{1}
. Nevertheless, we can rigorously support the preceding argument by working with the Lipschitz norm. This leads to the proof of the desired result.
∎

We finally recall the following classical result concerning bi-Lipschitz functions and measure theory.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
(\alpha,\beta)\in(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*})^{2}.
Consider
f:[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]\to\mathbb{R}
a bi-Lipschitz function such that

\forall(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]^{2},\quad|f(\lambda_{1})-f(\lambda_{2})|\geqslant\beta|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|.

Then, there exists
C>0
independent of
\|f\|_{\textnormal{Lip}}
such that

\Big|\big\{\lambda\in[\lambda_{*},\lambda^{*}]\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|f(\lambda)|\leqslant\alpha\big\}\Big|\leqslant C\tfrac{\alpha}{\beta}\cdot

Appendix A
Symplectic changes of coordinates and integral operators

This section’s major objective is to highlight useful findings related to some change of coordinates system. We refer the reader to the papers
[
8
,
11
,
35
,
71
]
for the proofs.
Let
\beta:\mathscr{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function such that
\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in\mathscr{O}}\|\beta(\lambda,\cdot,\centerdot)\|_{\textnormal{Lip}}<1

then there exists
\widehat{\beta}:\mathscr{O}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
smooth
such that

y=\theta+\beta(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\theta=y+\widehat{\beta}(\lambda,\varphi,y).

(A.1)

Define the operators

\mathscr{B}=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta)\mathrm{B},\qquad\mathrm{B}h(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)=h\big(\lambda,\varphi,\theta+\beta(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)\big).

(A.2)

Then

\mathscr{B}^{-1}=(1+\partial_{\theta}\widehat{\beta})\mathrm{B}^{-1},\qquad\mathrm{B}^{-1}h(\lambda,\varphi,y)=h\big(\lambda,\varphi,y+\widehat{\beta}(\lambda,\varphi,y)\big).

(A.3)

Now, we will provide some basic algebraic properties for the aforementioned operators.

Lemma A.1
.

The following assertions hold true.

1.

Let
\mathscr{B}_{1},\mathscr{B}_{2}
be two periodic change of variables as in (
A.2
), then

\mathscr{B}_{{1}}\mathscr{B}_{2}=(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta)\mathrm{B},

with

\beta(\varphi,\theta)\triangleq\beta_{1}(\varphi,\theta)+\beta_{2}\big(\varphi,\theta+\beta_{1}(\varphi,\theta)\big).

2.

The conjugation of the transport operator by
\mathscr{B}
keeps the same structure

\mathscr{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{\theta}\big(V(\varphi,\theta)\cdot\big)\Big)\mathscr{B}=\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}+\partial_{y}\big(\mathscr{V}(\varphi,y)\cdot\big),

with

\mathscr{V}(\varphi,y)\triangleq\,\mathrm{B}^{-1}\Big(\omega\cdot\partial_{\varphi}\beta(\varphi,\theta)+V(\varphi,\theta)\big(1+\partial_{\theta}\beta(\varphi,\theta)\big)\Big).

In what follows, and in the rest of this appendix, we assume that
(\lambda,s,s_{0})
satisfy (
7.3
) and we consider
\beta\in\textnormal{Lip}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
satisfying the smallness condition

\|\beta\|_{2s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\leqslant\varepsilon_{0},

with
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough.
The following result can be found in
[
35
,
11
]
.

Lemma A.2
.

The following assertions hold true.

1.

The linear operators
\mathrm{B},\mathscr{B}:\textnormal{Lip}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))\to\textnormal{Lip}_{\gamma}(\mathscr{O},H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
are continuous and invertible, with

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{B}^{\pm 1}h\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\leqslant\|h\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\left(1+C\|\beta\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\right)+C\|\beta\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|h\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{B}^{\pm 1}h\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\leqslant\|h\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\left(1+C\|\beta\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\right)+C\|\beta\|_{s+1}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|h\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}.

(A.5)

2.

The functions
\beta
and
\widehat{\beta}
defined through (
A.1
) satisfy the estimates

\|\widehat{\beta}\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\leqslant C\|\beta\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}.

The next result deals with some integral operator estimates. For the proof, we refer to
[
71
, Lem. 4.4]
and

[
12
, Lem. 2.3]
.

Lemma A.3
.

Consider a smooth real-valued kernel

K:(\lambda,\varphi,\theta,\eta)\mapsto K(\lambda,\varphi,\theta,\eta)

and let

(\mathcal{T}_{K}h)(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{T}}K(\lambda,\varphi,\theta,\eta)h(\lambda,\varphi,\eta)d\eta,

(A.6)

be an integral operator. Then, for any
s_{1},s_{2}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}h\|_{s_{1},s_{2}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|K\|_{s_{1}+s_{2}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}+\|h\|_{s_{1}}^{{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}}\|K\|_{s_{0}+s_{2}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}.

Given the periodic change of variables
\mathscr{B}
defined by (
A.2
).
Then, the operators
\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}\mathscr{B}
and
\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}\mathscr{B}-\mathcal{T}_{K}
are integral operators,

\displaystyle\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}\mathscr{B}\big)h(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}h(\lambda,\varphi,{\eta})\widehat{K}(\lambda,\varphi,\theta,{\eta})d{\eta}

\displaystyle\big(\mathscr{B}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}\mathscr{B}-\mathcal{T}_{K}\big)h(\lambda,\varphi,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}h(\lambda,\varphi,{\eta})\widetilde{K}(\lambda,\varphi,\theta,{\eta})d{\eta}

with

\displaystyle\|\widehat{K}\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}+\|K\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|\beta\|_{s+1}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)},

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{K}\|_{s}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|K\|_{s+1}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|\beta\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}+\|K\|_{s_{0}}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}\|\beta\|_{s+1}^{\textnormal{Lip}(\gamma)}.
